correspondence with
\pi
-calculus is not tight in that these systems
allow independent prefixes to be reordered.
Caires and Pfenning
[
CP10
]
give a propositions as
types correspondence between dual intuitionistic linear logic and a
session-typed
\pi
-calculus called
\pi\text{DILL}
.
They interpret tensor as output. The correspondence with

\pi
-calculus is tight in that independent prefixes may not be
reordered.
With CP
[
Wad14
]
, Wadler adapts
\pi\text{DILL}
to
classical linear logic.
Aschieri and Genco
[
AG20
]
give an interpretation of
classical multiplicative linear logic as concurrent functional
programs. They interpret
\invamp
as parallel composition, and the
connection to session types is less direct.

Priority-based Calculi

Systems such as
\pi\text{DILL}
, CP, and GV (and indeed HCP and HGV)
ensure deadlock freedom by exploiting the type system to statically
impose a tree structure on the communication topology — there can be
at most one communication channel between any two processes.
Another line of work explores a more liberal approach to deadlock
freedom enabling some cyclic communication topologies, where deadlock
freedom is guaranteed via
priorities
, which impose an order on
actions. Priorites were introduced by Kobayashi and Padovani

[
Kob06
,
Pad14
]
and adopted by Dardha and Gay

[
DG18
]
in Priority CP (PCP), and Kokke and Dardha in
Priority GV (PGV)
[
KD21b
]
.
Dezani
et al
.
[
DCdY07
]
and Vieira and Vasconcelos
[
VV13
]
use a partial order on channels to guarantee deadlock freedom, following Kobayashi’s work
[
Kob06
]
. Later on Dezani
et al
.
[
DCMYD06
]
guarantee progress by allowing only one active session at a time.
Carbone
et al
.
[
CDM14
]
use catalysers to show that progress is a compositional form of lock freedom for standard typed
\pi
calculus. The authors describe how this technique can be used for session typed
\pi
-calculus by using the the encoding of session types to linear types
[
DGS17
,
Dar14
,
Dar16
]
. Dardha and Perez
[
DP22
]
compare the different calculi and techniques for deadlock freedom using CP and CLL as a yardstick and showing that the class of processes in CP is strictly included in the class of processes typed by Kobayashi
[
Kob06
]
.

Graph-theoretic Approaches

Carbone and Debois
[
CD10
]
define a graph-theoretic approach for
a session typed
\pi
-calculus. They define an explicit dependency graph defined
inductively on the structure of a process, in contrast to our approach of
inducing a graph on type environments given a co-name set. They ensure
progress for processes with acyclic graphs using a
catalyser
, which provides a
missing counterpart to a process.
Jacobs
et al
.
[
JBK22a
]
also define a graph-theoretic approach to
deadlock freedom, but differently from Carbone and Debois, their work is based
on separation logic. A line of work on many-writer, single-reader process
calculi
[
Pad18
,
dP18
]
uses explicit dependency graphs to both
ensure resource separation and guarantee deadlock freedom, however it is not immediate how to apply this approach to functional calculi.

9.
Conclusion and future work

HGV exploits hypersequents to resolve fundamental modularity issues
with GV.
As a consequence, we have obtained a tight operational correspondence
between HGV and HCP.
HGV is a modular and extensible core calculus for functional
programming with
binary
session types.
In future we intend to apply hypersequents to
multiparty
versions of
CP
[
CLM+16
]
and GV
[
JBK22b
]
to exhibit a similarly strong
operational correspondence.

Appendices

Appendix A
Omitted Proofs for
Section

3
: Hypersequent GV

In this Appendix we give full definitions and proofs
for
Section

3
.

A.1.
Derived typing rules for syntactic sugar

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathbf{1}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M;N}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-Seq}\par\frac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\lambda().M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\mathbf{1}}\mathbin{\multimap}{T}}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-LamUnit}\par\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T^{\prime}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\lambda(x,y).M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{{T}\mathbin{\times}{T^{\prime}}}\mathbin{\multimap}{U}}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-LamPair}\par\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}\;x=M\;\mathbf{in}\;N}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-Let}\par\frac{}{\cdot\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{select}\;\mathbf{inl}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{{S}\mathbin{\oplus}{S^{\prime}}}\mathbin{\multimap}{S}}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-Select-Inl}\par\frac{}{\cdot\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{select}\;\mathbf{inr}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{{S}\mathbin{\oplus}{S^{\prime}}}\mathbin{\multimap}{S^{\prime}}}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-Select-Inr}\par\frac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}L}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{S}\mathbin{\with}{S^{\prime}}}}\quad{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}\quad{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\prime}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{offer}\;L\;\{\mathbf{inl}\;x\mapsto{M};\mathbf{inr}\;y\mapsto{N}\}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-Offer}\par\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}L}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\with}\{\}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Delta}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{offer}\;L\;\{\}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}\quad\textsc{lab=T-Offer-Absurd}

Figure 9
.
Derived rules for syntactic sugar

The main body makes use of syntactic sugar, and encodings of branching and
selection. Figure
9
shows the derived typing rules.

A.2.
Preservation Proof

Next, we detail the proof of preservation. We begin with the usual lemmas to
manipulate evaluation contexts, and the usual substitution lemma.

Lemma 24

(Subterm typeability)
.

Suppose
\mathbf{D}
is a derivation of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E[M]}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}
.
Then, there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}
,
a type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}
, and some subderivation
\mathbf{D}^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{D}
concluding

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}}
, where the position of
\mathbf{D}^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{D}

coincides with the position of the hole in
\mathbf{D}
.

Proof A.1
.

By induction on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E}
.

Lemma 25

(Replacement, Evaluation Contexts)
.

If:

•

\mathbf{D}
is a derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E}[{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}]}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}

•

\mathbf{D}^{\prime}
is a subderivation of
D
concluding

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}}

•

The position of
\textbf{D}^{\prime}
in
D
corresponds to that of
the hole in
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{3}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{3}}
is defined

then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{3}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E}[{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}]}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}
.

Proof A.2
.

By induction on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E}
.

Lemma 26

(Substitution)
.

If:

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}}

(3)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}
is defined

then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M\{N/x\}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}
.

Proof A.3
.

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}U}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}
.

Preservation of typing under term reduction is standard.

Lemma 27

(Preservation,
\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}
)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}
.

Proof A.4
.

A standard induction on the derivation of
\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}
.

Runtime type merging is commutative and associative. We make use of these
properties implicitly in the remainder of the proofs.

Lemma 28
.

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\sqcap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}\iff{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}\sqcap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\sqcap({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}\sqcap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{3}})\iff({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\sqcap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}})\sqcap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{3}}

Proof A.5
.

Immediate from the definition of
\sqcap
.

The first more major result is preservation of configuration typing under
structural congruence.

Lemma 29

(Preservation (
\equiv
))
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
.

Proof A.6
.

We consider the cases for the equivalence axioms; the congruence cases are
straightforward applications of the IH.

(
\LabTirName
SC-ParAssoc).

\begin{array}[]{r@{\quad}c@{\quad}l}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{1}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}\enskip{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{3}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{E}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{3}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{2}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{3}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}\parallel\mathcal{E}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}\sqcap R_{3}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{1}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{2}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{3}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}\parallel(\mathcal{D}\parallel\mathcal{E})}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}\sqcap R_{2}\sqcap R_{3}}}\hskip 9.24994pt&\hskip-10.0pt\iff\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt&\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{1}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\enskip{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{1}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}\parallel\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}\sqcap R_{2}}}\hskip 8.04996pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{3}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{E}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{3}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{1}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{2}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}_{3}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\mathcal{C}\parallel\mathcal{D})\parallel\mathcal{E}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}\sqcap R_{2}\sqcap R_{3}}}\end{array}

(

(where

\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\enskip{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\enskip{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}\parallel(\nu xy)\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}\sqcap R_{2}}}\\

Case
(
\LabTirName
SC-LinkComm).

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x{\overset{z}{\leftrightarrow}}y}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y{\overset{z}{\leftrightarrow}}x}

Assumption:

\frac{}{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x{\overset{z}{\leftrightarrow}}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}}

By dualising both variables, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{\overline{S}}}
.
Since duality is an involution, we can show
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\iff{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}
.

Thus:

\frac{}{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y{\overset{z}{\leftrightarrow}}x}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}}}

The reasoning for the symmetric case is identical.

The next result shows that configuration typeability is preserved under
configuration reduction. Note that this lemma makes crucial use of
Lemma
29
due to
\LabTirName
E-Equiv.

Lemma 30

(Preservation (
\longrightarrow
))
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
.

Proof A.7
.

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}
.
Where there is a choice for
\phi
, we prove the case for
\phi=\bullet
and
expand
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{T}[M]}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet(E[M])}
for some evaluation
context
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E}
; the
other cases are similar.

Case
(E-Reify-Fork).

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet E[\mathbf{fork}\;V]\longrightarrow(\nu xy)(\bullet E[x]\parallel\circ V\;y)}

Assumption:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E[\mathbf{fork}\;V]}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet E[\mathbf{fork}\;V]}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}

By Lemma
24
, there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}
such that

By Lemma

:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E[x]}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet E[x]}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}

7.3612pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet xx^{\prime})(x{\overset{z}{\leftrightarrow}}y\parallel\circ z^{\prime}\parallel\bullet M)}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}

By Lemma

,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M\{y/x^{\prime}\}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}
, thus:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M\{y/x^{\prime}\}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet M\{y/x^{\prime}\}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}

as required.

A.3.
Progress

Functional reduction satisfies progress: under an environment only containing
runtime names, a term will either reduce, be a value, or be ready to perform a
communication action.

Lemma 31

(Progress, Terms)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}
, then either
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}
is a value,
or there exists some
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}
, or
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}
can be written
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E[N]}
for some

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{fork}\;{V}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{send}\;{(V,W)}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{recv}\;{V}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{wait}\;{V}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{link}\;(V,W)}\}
.

Proof A.9
.

A standard induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}
.

Note that tree canonical forms can be defined inductively:

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}::={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\phi M}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\mathcal{A}\parallel\mathcal{F})}

We assume the same requirement for configurations
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}
as the
non-inductive definition of tree canonical forms: i.e., that for a
configuration
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\mathcal{A}\parallel\mathcal{F})}
, that
x\in\fv({\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}})
.

8
follows as a direct corollary of a slightly more
verbose property, which follows from the inductive definition of TCFs.

{defi}

[Open progress]
Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{\mathcal{F}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
, where
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}\not\longrightarrow
.
We say that
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}

satisfies open progress
if:

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xx^{\prime})(\mathcal{A}\parallel\mathcal{F}^{\prime}})
, where:

(a)

There exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{2}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{2}}

(b)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}
for some
session type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}
,
and
\mathsf{blocked}({\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}})
for some
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}\in\fv({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S})

(c)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
, where

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
satisfies open progress

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\phi M}
, and either
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}
is a value,
or
\mathsf{blocked}({\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\phi M}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}})
for some
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}\in\fv({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi})
.

Lemma 32

(Open progress)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}\not\longrightarrow
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}

satisfies open progress.

Proof A.10
.

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
.
By the definition of canonical forms, it must be the case that

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}

is of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\mathcal{A}\parallel\mathcal{F}^{\prime})}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet M}
.

We show the case where

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\circ M\parallel\mathcal{F}^{\prime})}
; the cases
for

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x{\overset{z}{\leftrightarrow}}x^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bullet M}
follow similar reasoning.

Assumption:

\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}\hskip 8.19447pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}\parallel\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\circ M\parallel\mathcal{F}^{\prime})}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}

In both cases, by the induction hypothesis,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}T}}
satisfies open progress.

Subcase
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ M}
).
By Lemma
31
, either
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}
is a value, or
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}
can be
written
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E[N]}
for some communication and concurrency construct
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N\in\{\mathbf{fork}\;{V},\mathbf{send}\;(V,W),\mathbf{recv}\;{V},\mathbf{wait}\;{V},}

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{link}\;(V,W)\}}
.

Otherwise,
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}
is a communication or concurrency construct. If
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{fork}\;V}
, then reduction could occur by
E-Reify-Fork
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{link}\;(V,W)}
, then by the type schema for
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{link}}
, we
have that
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{link}\;(V,W)}
must be of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{link}\;(z,z^{\prime})}
for

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z,z^{\prime}}\in\fv({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S})
and could reduce by

E-Reify-Link
.

Otherwise, it must be the case that
\mathsf{blocked}({\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ M}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z}})
for
some
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z}\in\fv({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S})
.

Thus,
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\circ M\parallel\mathcal{D})}
satisfies open progress, as
required.

Subcase
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{A}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z_{2}{\overset{z_{1}}{\leftrightarrow}}z_{3}}
).
We have that
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}}\in\fv({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Psi_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S})
, and the thread must be
blocked by definition.

Proof B.1
.

By induction on the derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}
.

Case
(
TG-New
).
Assumption:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{y,y^{\prime}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\sharp}}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu yy^{\prime})\mathcal{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}C}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}

Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S_{1}^{\sharp}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{x_{n},x^{\prime}_{n}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S_{n}^{\sharp}}

(for clarity, without loss of generality, we assume the
absence of non-session variables. As these are simply split between
environments, they can be added orthogonally).

By the IH, we have that there exists some hyper-environment
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
such
that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}
,
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
is a splitting of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{y,y^{\prime}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\sharp}}
.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
is a splitting of
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}
, we know that

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
, and that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
has a
tree structure with respect to names
\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{1}}\},\ldots,\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{n}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{n}}\},\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}\}\}
.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
has a tree structure, by definition we have that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}

for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}
, where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
has a tree structure.

By Lemma
5
(clause 1, left-to-right),

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}
has a tree structure with respect to names

\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{1}}\},\ldots,\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{n}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{n}}\}\}
.

Thus, we can show:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}){\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}

where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}
has a tree
structure with respect to names
\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{1}}\},\ldots,\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{n}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{n}}\}\}
and is therefore a
splitting of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}
, as required.

Case
(
TG-Connect
1
).
Assumption:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{y,y^{\prime}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\sharp}}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}\parallel\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\sqcap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}}

Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S_{1}^{\sharp}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{x_{m},x^{\prime}_{m}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S_{m}^{\sharp}}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{x_{m+1},x^{\prime}_{m+1}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S_{m+1}^{\sharp}},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{x_{n},x^{\prime}_{n}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S_{n}^{\sharp}}
.

By the IH, there exist hyper-environments
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}
such that:

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
is a splitting of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}
is a splitting of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}

(3)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}}

(4)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}

By the definition of splittings,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}
can be
written
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}}
for some

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}}
.
Furthermore,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}
has a tree structure with respectto

\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{1}}\},\ldots,\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{m}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{m}}\}\}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}
has a tree structure with respect to

\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{m+1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{m+1}}\},\ldots,\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{n}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{n}}\}\}
.

By Lemma
5
(clause 2, left-to-right),

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}^{\prime}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma^{\prime}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}

has a tree structure with respect to

\{\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{1}}\},\ldots,\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x_{n}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}_{n}}\},\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}\}\}

and therefore
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}
is a splitting of

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{y,y^{\prime}}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\sharp}}
.

Recomposing in HGV:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{C}}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{1}}\sqcap{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R_{2}}}}

as required.

Case
(
TG-Connect
2
).
Similar to
TG-Connect
1
.

Case
(
TG-Child
).
Assumption:

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathbf{end}_{!}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}\vdash_{\text{{GV}}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ M}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}}}

Since we mandated that variables of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\sharp}}
cannot appear in
terms, there are no names of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\sharp}}
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}
.
Therefore, the singleton hyper-environment
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}
is a valid
splitting, and so we can conclude by
TC-Child
in HGV.

Case
(
TG-Main
).
Similar to
TG-Child
.

See
14

Proof B.2
.

By induction on the number of
\nu
-bound names.

In the case that
n=0
, the result follows immediately by
TG-Child

or
TG-Main
.

In the case that
n\geq 1
, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}

for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}
and:

\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ L}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}}\hskip 8.19447pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\parallel{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ L\parallel\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\circ L\parallel\mathcal{D})}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}

such that
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}
is in tree canonical form.
That
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ L}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}}

follows by the definition of tree canonical forms, since
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}\in\fv({\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}L})
.

By the IH,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}
in GV.

Thus, we can write:

\frac{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ L}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\circ}}\hskip 8.19447pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\overline{S}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\langle{x,y}\rangle}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}S^{\sharp}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\circ L\parallel\mathcal{D}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu xy)(\circ L\parallel\mathcal{D})}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}R}}}

as required.

C.1.
Full definition of
\text{HGV}*

Syntax

\small\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\text{Terms}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}L},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}N}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}V}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}\;x=M\;\mathbf{in}\;N}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}V\;W}\\
&&\;\mid&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}\;()=V\;\mathbf{in}\;M}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}\;(x,y)=V\;\mathbf{in}\;M}\\
&&\;\mid&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{absurd}\;V}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{case}\;V\left\{\mathbf{inl}\;x\mapsto{M};\;\mathbf{inr}\;y\mapsto{N}\right\}}\\
\text{Values}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}V},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}W}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}K}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\lambda x.M}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}()}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(V,W)}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{inl}\;V}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{inr}\;V}\\
\text{Evaluation contexts}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}E}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\square}\;\mid\;{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}\;x=E\;\mathbf{in}\;M}\\
\text{Thread contexts}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}F}&\Coloneqq&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\phi\;E}\end{array}

9.24994pt\textsc{lab=TM*-CaseSum}

The typing of constants is the same as for HGV.

Operational Semantics

The operational semantics for
\text{HGV}*
is the same as for HGV
(Figure
4
), with the addition of the following explicit
rule for
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}}
:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\textsc{E-Let}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}\;x=V\;\mathbf{in}\;M}&\longrightarrow&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M\{V/x\}}\end{array}

Similarly,
\text{HGV}*
directly inherits HGV’s runtime typing.

C.2.
Translating
\text{HGV}*
to HCP

The translation is guaranteed to have only internal (i.e.,
\alpha
or

\beta
) transitions and transitions on the dedicated output channel.
More specifically:

Lemma 33
.

•

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{C}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{c}}}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell}}{\longrightarrow}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\alpha},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}\}
or
\ell=\ell_{r}
.

•

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell}}{\longrightarrow}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}
is
a non-value, then
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\alpha},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}\}
.

•

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}V}}
is a value, then

{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell_{r}}}{\longrightarrow}}}
.

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{v}}}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell}}{\longrightarrow}}
then
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\alpha},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}\}
.

Proof C.1
.

By induction on the structure of
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}
.

We do not use the above lemma directly, but it is a useful sanity
check.

{defi}

[process contexts]
A process context
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[\;]}
is a process with a single hole, denoted
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\square}
. We extend the typing rules, LTS and typing rules to process contexts. We write
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[\;]}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}}}
to mean that
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[\;]}}
is typed under hyper-environment
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}}
expecting a process typed under
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}}
,
i
.
e
., if
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}Q}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{G}}}}
then
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[Q]}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.4766}\mathcal{H}}}}
.

{defi}

A process
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P}
is
blocked on

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}
if it only has transitions
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\ell_{x}}}{\longrightarrow}}
.

Lemma 34
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[\;]}
is a process context with
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}w},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}w^{\prime}}\not\in\cn({\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[\;]})
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}Q}
is a process blocked on
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}w^{\prime}}
, then
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\parallel Q)}}\approx_{\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]}}
.

Proof C.2
.

By induction on the process context
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[\;]}
.

Case
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\square}
).

\begin{array}[]{lrll}\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){({z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}\parallel Q)}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\alpha}}{\longrightarrow}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}}\\
&\sim&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}}&\text{(by reflexivity)}\end{array}

Case
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{x}{y}){P[\;]}}
).

\begin{array}[]{lrll}\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){((\nu{x}{y}){(P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}])}\parallel Q)}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\sim&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{x}{y}){(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\parallel Q)}}}&\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hcp-sbis-to-bis})}\\
&\approx_{\alpha}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{x}{y}){(P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}])}}&\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hcp-sbis-to-bis} and IH)}\end{array}

Case
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[\;]\parallel R}
).

\begin{array}[]{lrll}\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\parallel R\parallel Q)}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\sim&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\parallel Q)}\parallel R}&\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hcp-sbis-to-bis})}\\
&\approx_{\alpha}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]\parallel R}&\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hcp-sbis-to-bis} and IH)}\end{array}

Case
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}R\parallel P[\;]}
).

\begin{array}[]{lrll}\lx@intercol{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(R\parallel P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\parallel Q)}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\sim&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}R\parallel(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\parallel Q)}}&\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hcp-sbis-to-bis})}\\
&\approx_{\alpha}&{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}R\parallel P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]}&\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hcp-sbis-to-bis} and IH)}\end{array}

Case
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\pi.{P[\;]}}
).
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}Q}
is blocked on
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}w^{\prime}}
, the process
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(\pi.{P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]}\parallel Q)}}
has only one transition,

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){(\pi.{P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]}\parallel Q)}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\pi}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){({P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]}\parallel Q)}}.

The process
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\pi.{P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]}}
has only one transition, also with label
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\pi}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\pi.{P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\pi}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]}}.

The resulting processes are bisimilar by the induction hypothesis.

Case
(
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\{\text{inl}:P[\;];\text{inr}:P^{\prime}[\;]\}}
).
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}Q}
is blocked on
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}w^{\prime}}
, the process
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){({x}\triangleright\{\text{inl}:P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}];\text{inr}:P^{\prime}[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\}\parallel Q)}}
has only two transitions,

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){({x}\triangleright\{\text{inl}:P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}];\text{inr}:P^{\prime}[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\}\parallel Q)}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\text{inl}}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){({P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]}\parallel Q)}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){({x}\triangleright\{\text{inl}:P[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}];\text{inr}:P^{\prime}[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]\}\parallel Q)}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\text{inr}}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{w}{w^{\prime}}){({P^{\prime}[{z}{\leftrightarrow}{w}]}\parallel Q)}}.

The process
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\{\text{inl}:P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}];\text{inr}:P^{\prime}[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]\}}
has only two transitions, also with labels
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\text{inl}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\text{inr}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\{\text{inl}:P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}];\text{inr}:P^{\prime}[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]\}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\text{inl}}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\{\text{inl}:P[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}];\text{inr}:P^{\prime}[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]\}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{x}\triangleright\text{inr}}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{P^{\prime}[Q\{z/w^{\prime}\}]}}.

The resulting processes are bisimilar by the induction hypothesis.

Part 3.

The cases are all by immediate induction.

Lemma 35
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}F}
be an
\text{HGV}*
evaluation context and
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}
a result
endpoint. Then there exists a process context
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{F}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{f}}}}
and a
result endpoint
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}v}=\mathsf{hr}({\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}F},{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r})
for the hole such that for all
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}
we
have that
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{F[M]}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{c}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{F}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{f}}}[\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}v}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}]}
.

Proof C.5
.

By induction on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}F}
.

In the above lemma, if
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}F}
is the empty context then
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}v}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}
. Otherwise
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}v}
is a variable bound by the process context

{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{F}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{f}}}}
.

Lemma 36

(Operational Correspondence, Terms)
.

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}
is a well-typed term:

(1)

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M^{\prime}}}
, then there exists a
P
such that

{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}\overset{\beta^{+}}{\Longrightarrow}_{\alpha}P}
and
{P\approx_{\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}}
; and

(2)

if
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P}}
, then there exists an

{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M^{\prime}}}
and a
{P^{\prime}}
such that

{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M^{\prime}}}
and
{P\overset{\beta^{*}}{\Longrightarrow}_{\alpha}P^{\prime}}

and
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P^{\prime}}\approx_{\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}}
.

Proof C.6
.

(1)

By induction on the reduction
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M^{\prime}}}
.

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\approx_{\alpha}\;\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hgv-to-hcp-substitution})}}}

Case
(
\LabTirName
E-Lift).
The induction hypothesis yields the reasoning steps depicted by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}}}

(2)

By induction on
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}

Case
(
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}U\;V}}
).
There are two well-typed cases for
{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}U}
: either
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}U}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z}}
for some
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z}}
; or
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}U}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\lambda{x}.M}}
for some
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M}}
. If
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}U}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}z}}
, we have
{{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{x}{x^{\prime}}){(\nu{y}{y^{\prime}}){(y\langle{x}\rangle.{r}{\leftrightarrow}{y}\parallel{z}{\leftrightarrow}{y^{\prime}}\parallel\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{v}}})}}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 6.0172pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 6.0172pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.60158pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.85158pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}}}
, which contradicts our premise. Therefore,
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}U}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\lambda{x}.M}}
. The only possible
\beta
-transition is the one in the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\lambda x.M)\;V}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{x}{x^{\prime}}){(\nu{y}{y^{\prime}}){(y\langle{x}\rangle.{{r}{\leftrightarrow}{y}}\parallel y^{\prime}(x).\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}\parallel\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{v}}})}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{x}{x^{\prime}}){(\nu{y}{y^{\prime}}){({{r}{\leftrightarrow}{y}}\parallel\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}y^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}\parallel\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{v}}})}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}{{(\nu{x}{x^{\prime}}){(\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}\parallel\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{v}}})}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M\{V/x\}}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\alpha}}{\longrightarrow}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\alpha}}{\longrightarrow}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\approx_{\alpha}}\;\text{(by Lemma~\ref{cor:hgv-to-hcp-substitution})}}

Hence,
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M\{V/x\}}}

, which contradicts our premise.

Case
(

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\mathbf{let}\;x=V\;\mathbf{in}\;M}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}(\nu{x}{x^{\prime}}){(\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}\parallel{\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{v}}}})}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M\{V/x\}}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}}

Otherwise, if
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\llbracket{M}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}x^{\prime}}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P}}

{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M^{\prime}}}
such that

and
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}P}\approx\llbracket{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}M^{\prime}}}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{m}}}}
. We apply HGV’s
\LabTirName
E-Lift and HCP’s
\LabTirName
Str-Res and
\LabTirName
Str-Par2.

Case
(
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}V}}
).
We have
{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\bar{r}.\llbracket{V}\rrbracket_{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}r}}^{\!\medspace{\mathsf{v}}}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 6.0172pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 6.0172pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 4.60158pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.85158pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}\beta}}{\longrightarrow}}}}}
, which contradicts our premise.

See
23

Proof C.7
.

(1)

.

(2)

Reflection of
\alpha
-transitions is trivial as

\alpha
-transition is included in
\alpha
-bisimulation.
Reflection of
\beta
-transitions is by induction on

{{\color[rgb]{0.6445,0,0.2188}C}}
; as with Lemma
36
, the
only well-typed
\beta
-transitions that can occur for each case
are those specified in the simulation case.
